  temper/docs/design/testing/reference-case.md:73 # The reference case | | 4.6 | `a_restarted_workstation_keeps_the_directors_question_and_answers_it_once` (`suite::fuzzy::case_4_6`), a cluster story of its own, so that each of the case's cluster stories keeps its margin under the real loop's patience on a busy machine: the case's third part, on ws, s1 and s2, all started and each free: the person opens a chat with the director, whose run is placed on ws, the first free slot, and waits in it; the director delegates A's next phase, an owner that runs on another worker and sends the director its question; the director reads it, a read committed by a completed turn (a recall of the project's notes), and waits again with its answer not yet sent; the person stops ws, whose ending (../protocol/cluster.md, 4) ends the director's run, failed, into a retry; after the case's backoff the retry is placed on a free worker, resumes from the chat's committed turns with A's question kept, and answers it once; A, told the answer, reports, and the director reports; then the person starts ws again on its own three directories, and it links at its position and runs nothing. The oracle: the director's chat ran two attempts, the first ended failed and the next began after it and finished, never two open, in the journal's order; the director's read of the question committed and its run waiting before ws stopped; the next attempt told the question once, one answer sent across both attempts, and A told it once; A's phase ended with its report. The story asserts what only the harness sees: the first attempt ran on ws and the next on s1 or s2; ws's two lives each linked once and drained, the second running nothing; every worker drained and no agent killed. Time keeps the case's order: an agent's failure backs off 1 ms in this story's fixture (`tasks.retries.agent.base_ms`), the shortest whole length the configuration takes and never zero: the retry's path is the same and only its wait is short, the backoff is not what the story tests, and the order comes from the engine, which journals the ended attempt before it places the next, the order the oracle reads; the profile's own backoff stays the engine's domain world's, and every other case story keeps it. ws's ending waits on events: the director's committed read (polled in its activity, since no `ctl` wait names a committed turn), its run waiting (`ctl task show TASK --until replied`, then its document read until its work is `waiting`, since replied also holds for a run still writing), then ws's ring settling. ws starts again once the chat has ended, so that the retry, placed by the cached workspace first, cannot go back to it.   temper/docs/design/testing/reference-case.md:73 # The reference case | | 4.6 | `a_restarted_workstation_keeps_the_directors_question_and_answers_it_once` (`suite::fuzzy::case_4_6`), a cluster story of its own, so that each of the case's cluster stories keeps its margin under the real loop's patience on a busy machine: the case's third part, on ws, s1 and s2, all started and each free: the person opens a chat with the director, whose run is placed on ws, the first free slot, and waits in it; the director delegates A's next phase, an owner that runs on another worker and sends the director its question; the director reads it, a read committed by a completed turn (a recall of the project's notes), and waits again with its answer not yet sent; the person stops ws, whose ending (../protocol/cluster.md, 4) ends the director's run, failed, into a retry; after the case's backoff the retry is placed on a free worker, resumes from the chat's committed turns with A's question kept, and answers it once; A, told the answer, reports, and the director reports; then the person starts ws again on its own three directories, and it links at its position and runs nothing. The oracle: the director's chat ran two attempts, the first ended failed and the next began after it and finished, never two open, in the journal's order; the director's read of the question committed and its run waiting before ws stopped; the next attempt told the question once, one answer sent across both attempts, and A told it once; A's phase ended with its report. The story asserts what only the harness sees: the first attempt ran on ws and the next on s1 or s2; ws's two lives each linked once and drained, the second running nothing; every worker drained and no agent killed. Time keeps the case's order: an agent's failure backs off 1 ms in this story's fixture (`tasks.retries.agent.base_ms`), the shortest whole length the configuration takes and never zero: the retry's path is the same and only its wait is short, the backoff is not what the story tests, and the order comes from the engine, which journals the ended attempt before it places the next, the order the oracle reads; the profile's own backoff stays the engine's domain world's, and every other case story keeps it. ws's ending waits on events: the director's committed read (polled in its activity, since no `ctl` wait names a committed turn), its run waiting (`ctl task show TASK --until replied`, then its document read until its work is `waiting`, since replied also holds for a run still writing), then ws's ring settling. ws starts again once the chat has ended, so that the retry, placed by the cached workspace first, cannot go back to it. the story asserts the first attempt on ws, by its live channel and ws's first life's mapping read while the run waits, before the stop; and the next on s1 or s2 by the acts' order, not a mapping: ws is ended from before the first attempt fails until after the chat has ended, so the retry, whose channel no read is sure to see before it ends (`process-tier.md`), is placed while s1 and s2 are the only workers linked; ws's two lives each linked once, and no forge token in any file or document the harness gives a worker (`process-tier.md`). ws's ending says it drains before its runs end, so jig's fleet places nothing on ws's channel while it is still linked, and no attempt of the retry is refused there (jig's `domain/engine/fleet.md`) | the hang past the grace, and its four hours (exact): here the link deadlines stay past the story's patience (`real-loop.md`), so no grace runs out, ws goes away by its ending rather than a hang, and the director's run ends failed as an agent's, not lost; its process story asserts it of the files and documents a worker is given, not of a git call's credential, which reaches the worker per call.   temper/docs/design/cli/temper-box-args.md:14 ### `temper-box` | - **Notes:** In a terminal, with --echo none, the box runs the TUI in front, signed in, with its link shown there. The block, --fake-backends lines and errors go to the box log while it runs; quitting the TUI stops the box.   temper/docs/design/cli/temper-box-args.md:14 ### `temper-box` | - **Notes:** In a terminal, with --echo none, the box runs the TUI in front, signed in, with its link shown there. The block, --fake-backends lines and errors go to the box log while it runs; quitting the TUI stops the box.
